## dost1001-0745

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A tangent and a chord meet at a point on a circle. The intercepted arc measures $150^\circ$. What is the angle between the tangent and chord?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $75^\circ$
  - **B:** $30^\circ$
  - **C:** $150^\circ$
  - **D:** $210^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($75^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
The angle formed by a tangent and a chord drawn to the point of tangency is equal to half the measure of its intercepted arc. Given that the intercepted arc measures $150^\circ$, the angle between the tangent and the chord is $\frac{1}{2} \times 150^\circ = 75^\circ$.

## dost1001-0746

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** What is the perpendicular distance from the point $(2, -1)$ to the line $3x + 4y - 12 = 0$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 1 unit
  - **B:** $\frac{10}{3}$ units
  - **C:** 2 units
  - **D:** 4 units
- **Answer Key:** **C** (2 units)

**Explanation:**
The perpendicular distance $D$ from a point $(x_1, y_1)$ to a line $Ax + By + C = 0$ is given by the formula $D = \frac{|Ax_1 + By_1 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}$. For the point $(2, -1)$ and the line $3x + 4y - 12 = 0$, we have $x_1 = 2$, $y_1 = -1$, $A = 3$, $B = 4$, and $C = -12$. Substituting these values, $D = \frac{|3(2) + 4(-1) - 12|}{\sqrt{3^2 + 4^2}} = \frac{|6 - 4 - 12|}{\sqrt{9 + 16}} = \frac{|-10|}{\sqrt{25}} = \frac{10}{5} = 2$ units.

---

## dost1001-0747

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** What is the centroid of the triangle with vertices $(0, 0)$, $(6, 0)$, and $(3, 9)$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(3, 3)$
  - **B:** $(3, 4.5)$
  - **C:** $(2, 3)$
  - **D:** $(9, 9)$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($(3, 3)$)

**Explanation:**
The centroid of a triangle with vertices $(x_1, y_1)$, $(x_2, y_2)$, and $(x_3, y_3)$ is given by the coordinates $\left(\frac{x_1 + x_2 + x_3}{3}, \frac{y_1 + y_2 + y_3}{3}\right)$. For the given vertices $(0, 0)$, $(6, 0)$, and $(3, 9)$, the centroid is $\left(\frac{0 + 6 + 3}{3}, \frac{0 + 0 + 9}{3}\right) = \left(\frac{9}{3}, \frac{9}{3}\right) = (3, 3)$.

---

## dost1001-0748

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A regular hexagon has side length 4 cm. What is its area?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $16\sqrt{3}\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **B:** $32\sqrt{3}\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **C:** $24\sqrt{3}\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **D:** $48\sqrt{3}\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($24\sqrt{3}\ \mathrm{cm}^2$)

**Explanation:**
A regular hexagon can be divided into six equilateral triangles. The area of an equilateral triangle with side length $s$ is given by the formula $A_{triangle} = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{4} s^2$. Since the side length of the regular hexagon is $4\ \text{cm}$, the side length of each equilateral triangle is also $4\ \text{cm}$. The area of the hexagon is $6 \times A_{triangle} = 6 \times \frac{\sqrt{3}}{4} (4\ \text{cm})^2 = 6 \times \frac{\sqrt{3}}{4} (16\ \text{cm}^2) = 6 \times 4\sqrt{3}\ \text{cm}^2 = 24\sqrt{3}\ \text{cm}^2$.

---

## dost1001-0749

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A solid cube has side length 6 cm. A square tunnel with cross section 2 cm by 2 cm is drilled straight through the cube from one face to the opposite face. What volume remains?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $24\ \mathrm{cm}^3$
  - **B:** $192\pi\ \mathrm{cm}^3$
  - **C:** $192\ \mathrm{cm}^3$
  - **D:** $216\ \mathrm{cm}^3$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($192\ \mathrm{cm}^3$)

**Explanation:**
The volume of the solid cube is $V_{cube} = (\text{side length})^3 = (6\ \text{cm})^3 = 216\ \text{cm}^3$. The volume of the square tunnel is $V_{tunnel} = (\text{cross-sectional area}) \times (\text{length})$. The cross-sectional area is $2\ \text{cm} \times 2\ \text{cm} = 4\ \text{cm}^2$, and the length of the tunnel is the side length of the cube, which is $6\ \text{cm}$. So, $V_{tunnel} = 4\ \text{cm}^2 \times 6\ \text{cm} = 24\ \text{cm}^3$. The volume of the remaining solid is $V_{remaining} = V_{cube} - V_{tunnel} = 216\ \text{cm}^3 - 24\ \text{cm}^3 = 192\ \text{cm}^3$.

## dost1001-0750

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A track is shaped like a rectangle 10 m long with a semicircle of radius 3 m attached at each short end. What is the perimeter of the outer boundary?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $10 + 6\pi$ m
  - **B:** $20 + 3\pi$ m
  - **C:** $20 + 12\pi$ m
  - **D:** $20 + 6\pi$ m
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($20 + 6\pi$ m)

**Explanation:**
The perimeter of the track consists of two straight sides of the rectangle and the circumferences of two semicircles. The length of the two straight sides is $2 \times 10 \text{ m} = 20 \text{ m}$. The two semicircles, each with radius $3 \text{ m}$, combine to form one full circle with circumference $2\pi r = 2\pi(3) = 6\pi \text{ m}$. Therefore, the total perimeter is $20 + 6\pi \text{ m}$.

## dost1001-0751

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** For the ellipse $\frac{x^2}{36} + \frac{y^2}{20} = 1$, where are the foci?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(\pm 4, 0)$
  - **B:** $(\pm\sqrt{20}, 0)$
  - **C:** $(0, \pm 4)$
  - **D:** $(\pm 6, 0)$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($(\pm 4, 0)$)

**Explanation:**
For an ellipse of the form $\frac{x^2}{a^2} + \frac{y^2}{b^2} = 1$ where $a^2 > b^2$, the foci are at $(\pm c, 0)$ where $c^2 = a^2 - b^2$. Here, $a^2 = 36$ and $b^2 = 20$, so $c^2 = 36 - 20 = 16$. Thus, $c = \sqrt{16} = 4$, and the foci are at $(\pm 4, 0)$.

---

## dost1001-0752

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** What are the asymptotes of the hyperbola $\frac{x^2}{16} - \frac{y^2}{9} = 1$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $y = \pm\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)x$
  - **B:** $y = \pm\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)x$
  - **C:** $y = \pm\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)x + 1$
  - **D:** $x = \pm 4$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($y = \pm\left(\frac{3}{4}\right)x$)

**Explanation:**
For a hyperbola of the form $\frac{x^2}{a^2} - \frac{y^2}{b^2} = 1$, the equations of the asymptotes are $y = \pm \frac{b}{a}x$. In this equation, $a^2 = 16$ and $b^2 = 9$, so $a = 4$ and $b = 3$. Therefore, the asymptotes are $y = \pm \frac{3}{4}x$.

## dost1001-0753

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Point $P(3, -2)$ is rotated $90^\circ$ counterclockwise about the origin and then reflected across the $x$-axis. What is the final image?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(-2, 3)$
  - **B:** $(-3, -2)$
  - **C:** $(2, -3)$
  - **D:** $(2, 3)$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($(2, -3)$)

**Explanation:**
A $90^\circ$ counterclockwise rotation about the origin transforms a point $(x, y)$ to $(-y, x)$. So, $P(3, -2)$ becomes $P'(-(-2), 3) = P'(2, 3)$. Reflecting a point $(x, y)$ across the $x$-axis transforms it to $(x, -y)$. Thus, $P'(2, 3)$ becomes $P''(2, -3)$.

## dost1001-0754

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** In a triangle, the segment joining the midpoints of two sides has length 9 cm. What is the length of the third side parallel to this segment?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 4.5 cm
  - **B:** 18 cm
  - **C:** 9 cm
  - **D:** 27 cm
- **Answer Key:** **C** (9 cm)

**Explanation:**
According to the Midpoint Theorem, the segment joining the midpoints of two sides of a triangle is parallel to the third side and is half the length of the third side. If the segment joining the midpoints has length $9 \text{ cm}$, then the length of the third side is $2 \times 9 \text{ cm} = 18 \text{ cm}$.

## dost1001-0755

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** In triangle ABC, points D on AB and E on AC satisfy $DE \parallel BC$. If AD = 4, DB = 6, and AE = 5, what is EC?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 2
  - **B:** 5
  - **C:** 12.5
  - **D:** 7.5
- **Answer Key:** **D** (7.5)

**Explanation:**
Since $DE \parallel BC$, by the Triangle Proportionality Theorem (also known as Thales's Theorem or Basic Proportionality Theorem), the sides are divided proportionally. Therefore, $\frac{AD}{DB} = \frac{AE}{EC}$. Substituting the given values, we have $\frac{4}{6} = \frac{5}{EC}$. Cross-multiplying gives $4 \times EC = 6 \times 5$, so $4 \times EC = 30$. Dividing by 4, $EC = \frac{30}{4} = 7.5$.

---

## dost1001-0756

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral. If $\angle A = 112^\circ$, what is $\angle C$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $68^\circ$
  - **B:** $56^\circ$
  - **C:** $112^\circ$
  - **D:** $248^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($68^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
In a cyclic quadrilateral, opposite angles are supplementary, meaning their sum is $180^\circ$. Therefore, $\angle A + \angle C = 180^\circ$. Given $\angle A = 112^\circ$, we have $112^\circ + \angle C = 180^\circ$. Solving for $\angle C$, we get $\angle C = 180^\circ - 112^\circ = 68^\circ$.

---

## dost1001-0757

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A convex polygon has 27 diagonals. How many sides does it have?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 7
  - **B:** 9
  - **C:** 8
  - **D:** 10
- **Answer Key:** **B** (9)

**Explanation:**
The number of diagonals $D$ in a convex polygon with $n$ sides is given by the formula $D = \frac{n(n-3)}{2}$. We are given $D = 27$, so $27 = \frac{n(n-3)}{2}$. Multiplying by 2 gives $54 = n(n-3)$, which expands to $n^2 - 3n - 54 = 0$. Factoring the quadratic equation, we get $(n-9)(n+6) = 0$. Since the number of sides cannot be negative, $n=9$.

---

## dost1001-0758

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** The endpoints of a diameter of a circle are $A(-2, 1)$ and $B(4, 5)$. Which equation represents the circle?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(x - 1)^2 + (y - 3)^2 = 52$
  - **B:** $(x + 1)^2 + (y + 3)^2 = 13$
  - **C:** $(x - 2)^2 + (y - 2)^2 = 13$
  - **D:** $(x - 1)^2 + (y - 3)^2 = 13$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($(x - 1)^2 + (y - 3)^2 = 13$)

**Explanation:**
The center of the circle is the midpoint of the diameter, $M = \left(\frac{-2+4}{2}, \frac{1+5}{2}\right) = (1, 3)$. The radius squared is the distance squared from the center to an endpoint, $r^2 = (4-1)^2 + (5-3)^2 = 3^2 + 2^2 = 9 + 4 = 13$. Thus, the equation of the circle is $(x - 1)^2 + (y - 3)^2 = 13$.

## dost1001-0759

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** At what point do the lines $y = 2x + 1$ and $y = -x + 7$ intersect?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(1, 3)$
  - **B:** $(2, 3)$
  - **C:** $(2, 5)$
  - **D:** $(3, 7)$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($(2, 5)$)

**Explanation:**
To find the intersection point, set the two equations equal to each other: $2x + 1 = -x + 7$. Solving for $x$, we get $3x = 6$, so $x = 2$. Substitute $x = 2$ into either equation to find $y$: $y = 2(2) + 1 = 5$. Therefore, the intersection point is $(2, 5)$.

## dost1001-0760

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A circular ring has outer radius 5 cm and inner radius 3 cm. What is the area of the ring?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $8\pi\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **B:** $16\pi\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **C:** $25\pi\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **D:** $34\pi\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($16\pi\ \mathrm{cm}^2$)

**Explanation:**
The area of the circular ring is the area of the outer circle minus the area of the inner circle. Given outer radius $R = 5\ \mathrm{cm}$ and inner radius $r = 3\ \mathrm{cm}$, the area is $A = \pi R^2 - \pi r^2 = \pi (5^2) - \pi (3^2) = 25\pi - 9\pi = 16\pi\ \mathrm{cm}^2$.

---

## dost1001-0761

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Two similar solids have corresponding linear dimensions in the ratio $3:1$. What is the ratio of their volumes, larger to smaller?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $27:1$
  - **B:** $3:1$
  - **C:** $9:1$
  - **D:** $81:1$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($27:1$)

**Explanation:**
For similar solids, if the ratio of their corresponding linear dimensions is $a:b$, then the ratio of their volumes is $a^3:b^3$. Given the linear dimension ratio is $3:1$, the ratio of their volumes is $3^3:1^3 = 27:1$.

## dost1001-0762

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** In triangle ABC, the internal angle bisectors meet at I. If $\angle A = 50^\circ$, what is $\angle BIC$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $65^\circ$
  - **B:** $105^\circ$
  - **C:** $115^\circ$
  - **D:** $130^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($115^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
The point $I$ is the incenter, where the angle bisectors meet. The formula for the angle $\angle BIC$ in terms of $\angle A$ is $\angle BIC = 90^\circ + \frac{1}{2}\angle A$. Given $\angle A = 50^\circ$, we have $\angle BIC = 90^\circ + \frac{1}{2}(50^\circ) = 90^\circ + 25^\circ = 115^\circ$.

---

## dost1001-0763

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** In triangle ABC, $DE \parallel BC$ with D on AB and E on AC. If $\frac{AD}{AB} = \frac{3}{5}$ and the area of triangle ABC is $200\ \mathrm{cm}^2$, what is the area of trapezoid DBCE?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $72\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **B:** $128\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **C:** $120\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **D:** $160\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($128\ \mathrm{cm}^2$)

**Explanation:**
Since $DE \parallel BC$, triangle $ADE$ is similar to triangle $ABC$. The ratio of their corresponding sides is $\frac{AD}{AB} = \frac{3}{5}$. The ratio of their areas is the square of the ratio of their sides, so $\frac{\text{Area}(\triangle ADE)}{\text{Area}(\triangle ABC)} = \left(\frac{3}{5}\right)^2 = \frac{9}{25}$. Given $\text{Area}(\triangle ABC) = 200\ \mathrm{cm}^2$, $\text{Area}(\triangle ADE) = \frac{9}{25} \times 200 = 9 \times 8 = 72\ \mathrm{cm}^2$. The area of trapezoid $DBCE$ is $\text{Area}(\triangle ABC) - \text{Area}(\triangle ADE) = 200 - 72 = 128\ \mathrm{cm}^2$.

---

## dost1001-0764

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** In a regular polygon, each interior angle is five times each exterior angle. How many sides does the polygon have?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 6
  - **B:** 12
  - **C:** 10
  - **D:** 15
- **Answer Key:** **B** (12)

**Explanation:**
Let the exterior angle be $x$. Then the interior angle is $5x$. Since the sum of an interior and exterior angle is $180^\circ$, we have $x + 5x = 180^\circ$, which means $6x = 180^\circ$, so $x = 30^\circ$. For a regular polygon, the measure of an exterior angle is $\frac{360^\circ}{n}$, where $n$ is the number of sides. Thus, $30^\circ = \frac{360^\circ}{n}$, which gives $n = \frac{360}{30} = 12$.

---

## dost1001-0765

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Two secants intersect outside a circle. The larger intercepted arc measures $170^\circ$ and the smaller intercepted arc measures $70^\circ$. What is the measure of the exterior angle formed by the secants?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $40^\circ$
  - **B:** $100^\circ$
  - **C:** $50^\circ$
  - **D:** $120^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($50^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
When two secants intersect outside a circle, the measure of the exterior angle formed is half the difference of the measures of the intercepted arcs. Given the larger arc is $170^\circ$ and the smaller arc is $70^\circ$, the exterior angle is $\frac{1}{2}(170^\circ - 70^\circ) = \frac{1}{2}(100^\circ) = 50^\circ$.

---

## dost1001-0766

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Two congruent circles each have radius 5 cm, and their centers are 8 cm apart. The circles intersect at two points. What is the length of their common chord?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 3 cm
  - **B:** 6 cm
  - **C:** 8 cm
  - **D:** 10 cm
- **Answer Key:** **B** (6 cm)

**Explanation:**
The common chord of two intersecting circles is perpendicular to the line connecting their centers and is bisected by it. Since the circles are congruent with radius $r=5$ cm and their centers are $8$ cm apart, the distance from each center to the midpoint of the common chord is $8/2 = 4$ cm. Using the Pythagorean theorem, half the length of the common chord is $\sqrt{r^2 - 4^2} = \sqrt{5^2 - 4^2} = \sqrt{25 - 16} = \sqrt{9} = 3$ cm. Therefore, the full length of the common chord is $2 \times 3 = 6$ cm.

---

## dost1001-0767

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Points $A(2, 1)$ and $B(8, 5)$ are fixed. Which equation represents the locus of points equidistant from $A$ and $B$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $3x + 2y = 21$
  - **B:** $2x + 3y = 19$
  - **C:** $3x - 2y = 9$
  - **D:** $2x - 3y = 1$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($3x + 2y = 21$)

**Explanation:**
The locus of points equidistant from two fixed points $A$ and $B$ is the perpendicular bisector of the segment $AB$. First, find the midpoint $M$ of $AB$: $M = \left(\frac{2+8}{2}, \frac{1+5}{2}\right) = (5, 3)$. Next, find the slope of $AB$: $m_{AB} = \frac{5-1}{8-2} = \frac{4}{6} = \frac{2}{3}$. The slope of the perpendicular bisector will be the negative reciprocal of $m_{AB}$, so $m_{\perp} = -\frac{3}{2}$. Using the point-slope form $y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)$ with point $M(5, 3)$ and slope $m_{\perp} = -\frac{3}{2}$, we get $y - 3 = -\frac{3}{2}(x - 5)$. Multiply by 2: $2(y - 3) = -3(x - 5) \Rightarrow 2y - 6 = -3x + 15 \Rightarrow 3x + 2y = 21$.

---

## dost1001-0768

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Point $P(t, t + 1)$ lies on the circle $x^2 + y^2 = 25$. If $t > 0$, what is $t$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 2
  - **B:** 4
  - **C:** 3
  - **D:** 5
- **Answer Key:** **C** (3)

**Explanation:**
Substitute the coordinates of point $P(t, t+1)$ into the equation of the circle $x^2 + y^2 = 25$. This gives $t^2 + (t+1)^2 = 25$. Expanding the equation yields $t^2 + (t^2 + 2t + 1) = 25$, which simplifies to $2t^2 + 2t + 1 = 25$. Rearranging into a quadratic equation gives $2t^2 + 2t - 24 = 0$, or $t^2 + t - 12 = 0$. Factoring the quadratic equation, we get $(t+4)(t-3) = 0$. The possible values for $t$ are $t = -4$ or $t = 3$. Since the problem states $t > 0$, the correct value is $t = 3$.

---

## dost1001-0769

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A circle has radius 4 cm. What is the area of the minor circular segment cut off by a chord that subtends a $90^\circ$ central angle?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $4\pi + 8\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **B:** $4\pi - 8\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **C:** $8\pi - 4\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
  - **D:** $16\pi - 8\ \mathrm{cm}^2$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($4\pi - 8\ \mathrm{cm}^2$)

**Explanation:**
The area of a minor circular segment is the area of the circular sector minus the area of the triangle formed by the two radii and the chord. For a radius $r=4$ cm and a central angle $\theta = 90^\circ = \frac{\pi}{2}$ radians, the area of the sector is $A_{sector} = \frac{1}{2}r^2\theta = \frac{1}{2}(4^2)\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right) = \frac{1}{2}(16)\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right) = 4\pi\ \mathrm{cm}^2$. The area of the triangle formed by the two radii and the chord is $A_{triangle} = \frac{1}{2}r^2\sin\theta = \frac{1}{2}(4^2)\sin(90^\circ) = \frac{1}{2}(16)(1) = 8\ \mathrm{cm}^2$. Therefore, the area of the minor circular segment is $A_{segment} = A_{sector} - A_{triangle} = (4\pi - 8)\ \mathrm{cm}^2$.

---

## dost1001-0770

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A solid sphere of radius 6 cm is melted and recast into 27 identical smaller solid spheres with no material lost. What is the radius of each smaller sphere?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0770.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 1 cm
  - **B:** 3 cm
  - **C:** $\frac{6}{\sqrt{27}}$ cm
  - **D:** 2 cm
- **Answer Key:** **D** (2 cm)

**Explanation:**
The volume of a sphere is given by the formula $V = \frac{4}{3}\pi r^3$. The volume of the large sphere with radius $R=6$ cm is $V_{large} = \frac{4}{3}\pi (6^3) = \frac{4}{3}\pi (216)$. When this sphere is melted and recast into 27 identical smaller spheres, the total volume remains the same. Let $r$ be the radius of each smaller sphere. The total volume of the 27 smaller spheres is $27 \times \left(\frac{4}{3}\pi r^3\right)$. Equating the volumes: $\frac{4}{3}\pi (216) = 27 \times \frac{4}{3}\pi r^3$. We can cancel $\frac{4}{3}\pi$ from both sides: $216 = 27r^3$. Dividing by 27, we get $r^3 = \frac{216}{27} = 8$. Taking the cube root of both sides, $r = \sqrt[3]{8} = 2$ cm.

---

## dost1001-0771

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A rectangular box has dimensions 3 cm by 4 cm by 12 cm. What is the length of the space diagonal joining opposite vertices?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 12 cm
  - **B:** $\sqrt{137}$ cm
  - **C:** 13 cm
  - **D:** 19 cm
- **Answer Key:** **C** (13 cm)

**Explanation:**
The length of the space diagonal ($d$) of a rectangular box with dimensions length ($l$), width ($w$), and height ($h$) is given by the formula $d = \sqrt{l^2 + w^2 + h^2}$. For a box with dimensions 3 cm, 4 cm, and 12 cm, we have $l=3$, $w=4$, and $h=12$. Substituting these values into the formula: $d = \sqrt{3^2 + 4^2 + 12^2} = \sqrt{9 + 16 + 144} = \sqrt{25 + 144} = \sqrt{169}$. Therefore, the length of the space diagonal is $d = 13$ cm.

---

## dost1001-0772

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** For the parabola $(x - 2)^2 = 12(y + 1)$, what is its focus?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(2, 2)$
  - **B:** $(2, -4)$
  - **C:** $(5, -1)$
  - **D:** $(-1, 2)$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($(2, 2)$)

**Explanation:**
The given equation of the parabola is $(x - 2)^2 = 12(y + 1)$. This is in the standard form $(x - h)^2 = 4p(y - k)$, where $(h, k)$ is the vertex and $p$ is the distance from the vertex to the focus. From the equation, the vertex is $(h, k) = (2, -1)$. Comparing $4p = 12$, we find $p = 3$. Since the $x$ term is squared, the parabola opens vertically. For a parabola opening upwards (as $4p$ is positive), the focus is at $(h, k + p)$. Therefore, the focus is $(2, -1 + 3) = (2, 2)$.

---

## dost1001-0773

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** For the hyperbola $\frac{x^2}{9} - \frac{y^2}{16} = 1$, where are the foci?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(\pm 3, 0)$
  - **B:** $(\pm 5, 0)$
  - **C:** $(0, \pm 5)$
  - **D:** $(\pm 4, 0)$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($(\pm 5, 0)$)

**Explanation:**
The given equation of the hyperbola is $\frac{x^2}{9} - \frac{y^2}{16} = 1$. This is in the standard form $\frac{x^2}{a^2} - \frac{y^2}{b^2} = 1$, which indicates a horizontal hyperbola centered at the origin. From the equation, $a^2 = 9 \Rightarrow a = 3$ and $b^2 = 16 \Rightarrow b = 4$. For a hyperbola, the distance from the center to each focus, $c$, is related by the equation $c^2 = a^2 + b^2$. Substituting the values, $c^2 = 9 + 16 = 25$, so $c = 5$. Since it's a horizontal hyperbola centered at the origin, the foci are at $(\pm c, 0)$. Therefore, the foci are at $(\pm 5, 0)$.

---

## dost1001-0774

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Point $P(-1, 4)$ is rotated $90^\circ$ clockwise about the origin and then translated by $(-3, 2)$. What is the final image?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(-7, 1)$
  - **B:** $(3, 1)$
  - **C:** $(1, 3)$
  - **D:** $(1, -3)$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($(1, 3)$)

**Explanation:**
A $90^\circ$ clockwise rotation about the origin transforms a point $(x, y)$ to $(y, -x)$. Thus, $P(-1, 4)$ becomes $P'(4, -(-1)) = (4, 1)$. Next, translating $P'(4, 1)$ by $(-3, 2)$ means adding the translation vector to the coordinates: $(4 + (-3), 1 + 2) = (1, 3)$.

---

## dost1001-0775

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** In a right triangle, the altitude from the right angle to the hypotenuse divides the hypotenuse into segments 4 cm and 9 cm. What is the length of the leg adjacent to the 4-cm segment?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 6 cm
  - **B:** $\sqrt{13}$ cm
  - **C:** $4\sqrt{13}$ cm
  - **D:** $2\sqrt{13}$ cm
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($2\sqrt{13}$ cm)

**Explanation:**
Let the hypotenuse be $c = 4 + 9 = 13$ cm. Let the leg adjacent to the 4-cm segment be $a$. By the geometric mean theorem (leg rule), the length of a leg is the geometric mean of the hypotenuse and the segment of the hypotenuse adjacent to that leg. So, $a^2 = 4 \times 13 = 52$. Therefore, $a = \sqrt{52} = \sqrt{4 \times 13} = 2\sqrt{13}$ cm.

---

## dost1001-0776

- **Topic:** geometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** In acute triangle ABC, the altitudes intersect at H. If $\angle BHC = 124^\circ$, what is $\angle A$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $34^\circ$
  - **B:** $62^\circ$
  - **C:** $56^\circ$
  - **D:** $124^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($56^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
Let the altitudes from B and C be $BD$ and $CE$ respectively, intersecting at $H$. Consider quadrilateral $AEHD$. Since $AEH$ and $ADH$ are right angles (altitudes), the sum of angles in $AEHD$ is $360^\circ$. Thus, $\angle A + \angle EHD = 180^\circ$. Since $\angle BHC$ and $\angle EHD$ are vertically opposite angles, $\angle EHD = \angle BHC = 124^\circ$. Therefore, $\angle A = 180^\circ - 124^\circ = 56^\circ$.

---

## dost1001-0777

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** In a right triangle, relative to acute angle $\theta$, the side opposite $\theta$ is 6 cm and the hypotenuse is 10 cm. What is $\sin\theta$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\frac{4}{5}$
  - **B:** $\frac{3}{4}$
  - **C:** $\frac{3}{5}$
  - **D:** $\frac{5}{3}$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($\frac{3}{5}$)

**Explanation:**
The sine of an acute angle $\theta$ in a right triangle is defined as the ratio of the length of the side opposite $\theta$ to the length of the hypotenuse. Given the opposite side is 6 cm and the hypotenuse is 10 cm, $\sin\theta = \frac{\text{opposite}}{\text{hypotenuse}} = \frac{6}{10} = \frac{3}{5}$.

---

## dost1001-0778

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** A 3–4–5 right triangle has angle $\alpha$ opposite the side of length 3. What is $\cos\alpha$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\frac{3}{5}$
  - **B:** $\frac{3}{4}$
  - **C:** $\frac{4}{5}$
  - **D:** $\frac{5}{4}$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($\frac{4}{5}$)

**Explanation:**
In a 3-4-5 right triangle, the side opposite angle $\alpha$ is 3, the adjacent side is 4, and the hypotenuse is 5. The cosine of angle $\alpha$ is defined as the ratio of the length of the side adjacent to $\alpha$ to the length of the hypotenuse. Therefore, $\cos\alpha = \frac{\text{adjacent}}{\text{hypotenuse}} = \frac{4}{5}$.

---

## dost1001-0779

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** In a right triangle, the side opposite angle $\beta$ is 8 m and the adjacent leg is 15 m. What is $\tan\beta$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\frac{15}{8}$
  - **B:** $\frac{8}{15}$
  - **C:** $\frac{8}{17}$
  - **D:** $\frac{15}{17}$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($\frac{8}{15}$)

**Explanation:**
The tangent of an angle $\beta$ in a right triangle is defined as the ratio of the length of the side opposite $\beta$ to the length of the side adjacent to $\beta$. Given the opposite side is 8 m and the adjacent leg is 15 m, $\tan\beta = \frac{\text{opposite}}{\text{adjacent}} = \frac{8}{15}$.

## dost1001-0780

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** A right triangle has hypotenuse 12 cm and an acute angle of $60^\circ$. What is the length of the leg adjacent to the $60^\circ$ angle?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0780.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $6\sqrt{3}\ \text{cm}$
  - **B:** 6 cm
  - **C:** $12\sqrt{3}\ \text{cm}$
  - **D:** 24 cm
- **Answer Key:** **B** (6 cm)

**Explanation:**
Let the hypotenuse be $c = 12$ cm and the acute angle be $\theta = 60^\circ$. The length of the leg adjacent to the $60^\circ$ angle, let's call it $x$, can be found using the cosine function: $\cos\theta = \frac{\text{adjacent}}{\text{hypotenuse}}$. So, $\cos 60^\circ = \frac{x}{12}$. Since $\cos 60^\circ = \frac{1}{2}$, we have $\frac{1}{2} = \frac{x}{12}$, which implies $x = 12 \times \frac{1}{2} = 6$ cm.

## dost1001-0781

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** What is $\sin 150^\circ$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $-\frac{1}{2}$
  - **B:** $\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$
  - **C:** $\frac{1}{2}$
  - **D:** $-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($\frac{1}{2}$)

**Explanation:**
The angle $150^\circ$ is in the second quadrant. The reference angle is $180^\circ - 150^\circ = 30^\circ$. In the second quadrant, the sine function is positive. Therefore, $\sin 150^\circ = \sin 30^\circ = \frac{1}{2}$.

## dost1001-0782

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** Which statement is true for an angle of $225^\circ$ in standard position?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0782.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** Sine is positive and cosine is negative.
  - **B:** Sine is negative and cosine is positive.
  - **C:** Both sine and cosine are negative.
  - **D:** Both sine and cosine are positive.
- **Answer Key:** **C** (Both sine and cosine are negative.)

**Explanation:**
An angle of $225^\circ$ lies in the third quadrant, as it is greater than $180^\circ$ but less than $270^\circ$. In the third quadrant, the x-coordinates (related to cosine) are negative and the y-coordinates (related to sine) are also negative. Therefore, both sine and cosine are negative.

## dost1001-0783

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** Convert $60^\circ$ to radians.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\frac{\pi}{6}$
  - **B:** $\frac{\pi}{3}$
  - **C:** $\frac{2\pi}{3}$
  - **D:** $3\pi$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($\frac{\pi}{3}$)

**Explanation:**
To convert degrees to radians, multiply the degree measure by the conversion factor $\frac{\pi \text{ radians}}{180^\circ}$. Thus, $60^\circ \times \frac{\pi}{180^\circ} = \frac{60\pi}{180} = \frac{\pi}{3}$ radians.

## dost1001-0784

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** Which angle is coterminal with $40^\circ$?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0784.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $140^\circ$
  - **B:** $400^\circ$
  - **C:** $220^\circ$
  - **D:** $320^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($400^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
Coterminal angles share the same terminal side and differ by an integer multiple of $360^\circ$. Adding $360^\circ$ to $40^\circ$ gives $40^\circ + 360^\circ = 400^\circ$, which is coterminal with $40^\circ$.

## dost1001-0785

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** Which expression is identically equal to 1?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\sin^2 x + \cos^2 x$
  - **B:** $\sin x + \cos x$
  - **C:** $\tan^2 x + 1$
  - **D:** $\sec^2 x - 1$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($\sin^2 x + \cos^2 x$)

**Explanation:**
The fundamental Pythagorean identity in trigonometry states that $\sin^2 x + \cos^2 x = 1$ for all values of $x$. This identity is derived from the Pythagorean theorem applied to a right triangle in the unit circle.

## dost1001-0786

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** Which reciprocal identity is correct?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\sec x = \frac{1}{\sin x}$
  - **B:** $\csc x = \frac{1}{\cos x}$
  - **C:** $\sec x = \frac{1}{\cos x}$
  - **D:** $\cot x = \frac{1}{\tan^2 x}$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($\sec x = \frac{1}{\cos x}$)

**Explanation:**
The reciprocal identities define the relationships between the six trigonometric functions. Specifically, the secant function is the reciprocal of the cosine function, meaning $\sec x = \frac{1}{\cos x}$.

## dost1001-0787

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** Solve $\sin x = 1$ for $0 \le x < 2\pi$.

- **Answer Key:** **D** ($x = \frac{\pi}{2}$)

**Explanation:**
For the equation $\sin x = 1$, we are looking for an angle $x$ in the interval $0 \le x < 2\pi$ where the y-coordinate on the unit circle is 1. This occurs at the angle $x = \frac{\pi}{2}$ radians, which corresponds to $90^\circ$.

## dost1001-0788

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** What is the amplitude of $y = 3\sin x$?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0788.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 1
  - **B:** $2\pi$
  - **C:** 3
  - **D:** 6
- **Answer Key:** **C** (3)

**Explanation:**
For a sinusoidal function of the form $y = A\sin(Bx - C) + D$, the amplitude is given by the absolute value of $A$. In the given function $y = 3\sin x$, $A = 3$, so the amplitude is $|3| = 3$.

## dost1001-0789

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 1
- **Question:** In triangle $ABC$, $A = 30^\circ$, $B = 90^\circ$, and side $a$ opposite $A$ is 5 cm. Using the Law of Sines, what is side $b$ opposite $B$?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0789.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $5\sqrt{3}\ \text{cm}$
  - **B:** 10 cm
  - **C:** 15 cm
  - **D:** 20 cm
- **Answer Key:** **B** (10 cm)

**Explanation:**
The Law of Sines states that $\frac{a}{\sin A} = \frac{b}{\sin B} = \frac{c}{\sin C}$. Given $A = 30^\circ$, $B = 90^\circ$, and $a = 5$ cm, we can set up the proportion $\frac{5}{\sin 30^\circ} = \frac{b}{\sin 90^\circ}$. Since $\sin 30^\circ = \frac{1}{2}$ and $\sin 90^\circ = 1$, we have $\frac{5}{1/2} = \frac{b}{1}$, which simplifies to $10 = b$. Thus, side $b$ is 10 cm.

---

## dost1001-0790

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** A hiker walks 3 km east and then 4 km north. What is the magnitude of the displacement from the starting point?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0790.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 1 km
  - **B:** 7 km
  - **C:** 5 km
  - **D:** 12 km
- **Answer Key:** **C** (5 km)

**Explanation:**
The hiker's path forms a right-angled triangle where the eastward and northward movements are the legs. The magnitude of the displacement is the hypotenuse, calculated using the Pythagorean theorem: $d = \sqrt{(3\ \text{km})^2 + (4\ \text{km})^2} = \sqrt{9\ \text{km}^2 + 16\ \text{km}^2} = \sqrt{25\ \text{km}^2} = 5\ \text{km}$.

---

## dost1001-0791

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** From a point on level ground, the angle of elevation to the top of a 20-m building is $35^\circ$. Which expression gives the horizontal distance $d$ from the point to the building?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $d = 20\tan 35^\circ\ \text{m}$
  - **B:** $d = \frac{20}{\tan 35^\circ}\ \text{m}$
  - **C:** $d = \frac{20}{\sin 35^\circ}\ \text{m}$
  - **D:** $d = 20\cos 35^\circ\ \text{m}$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($d = \frac{20}{\tan 35^\circ}\ \text{m}$)

**Explanation:**
In the right triangle formed by the building, the ground, and the line of sight, the height of the building (20 m) is the side opposite the $35^\circ$ angle, and the horizontal distance $d$ is the side adjacent. The tangent function relates these: $\tan 35^\circ = \frac{\text{opposite}}{\text{adjacent}} = \frac{20}{d}$. Solving for $d$ gives $d = \frac{20}{\tan 35^\circ}\ \text{m}$.

---

## dost1001-0792

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** A 10-m ladder leans against a vertical wall and makes a $65^\circ$ angle with the ground. How high up the wall does the ladder reach?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $10\cos 65^\circ\ \text{m}$
  - **B:** $10\sin 65^\circ\ \text{m}$
  - **C:** $10\tan 65^\circ\ \text{m}$
  - **D:** $\frac{10}{\sin 65^\circ}\ \text{m}$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($10\sin 65^\circ\ \text{m}$)

**Explanation:**
The ladder, wall, and ground form a right triangle. The length of the ladder (10 m) is the hypotenuse, and the height the ladder reaches up the wall is the side opposite the $65^\circ$ angle. The sine function relates these: $\sin 65^\circ = \frac{\text{opposite}}{\text{hypotenuse}} = \frac{\text{height}}{10\ \text{m}}$. Therefore, the height is $10\sin 65^\circ\ \text{m}$.

## dost1001-0793

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** A right isosceles triangle has legs 7 cm each. What is the length of its hypotenuse?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $7\sqrt{2}\ \text{cm}$
  - **B:** 14 cm
  - **C:** $\frac{7}{\sqrt{2}}\ \text{cm}$
  - **D:** 49 cm
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($7\sqrt{2}\ \text{cm}$)

**Explanation:**
For a right isosceles triangle, the two legs are equal in length. Using the Pythagorean theorem, if the legs are $a=7\ \text{cm}$ and $b=7\ \text{cm}$, the hypotenuse $c$ is $c = \sqrt{a^2 + b^2} = \sqrt{7^2 + 7^2} = \sqrt{49 + 49} = \sqrt{98} = \sqrt{49 \times 2} = 7\sqrt{2}\ \text{cm}$.

---

## dost1001-0794

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** What is $\cos\left(\frac{5\pi}{3}\right)$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $-\frac{1}{2}$
  - **B:** $\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$
  - **C:** $\frac{1}{2}$
  - **D:** $-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($\frac{1}{2}$)

**Explanation:**
The angle $\frac{5\pi}{3}$ radians is equivalent to $300^\circ$. This angle is in the fourth quadrant, where cosine values are positive. The reference angle for $300^\circ$ is $360^\circ - 300^\circ = 60^\circ$, so $\cos\left(\frac{5\pi}{3}\right) = \cos(300^\circ) = \cos(60^\circ) = \frac{1}{2}$.

## dost1001-0795

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** What is the reference angle for $210^\circ$?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0795.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $60^\circ$
  - **B:** $150^\circ$
  - **C:** $210^\circ$
  - **D:** $30^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($30^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
The angle $210^\circ$ is in the third quadrant. To find the reference angle, subtract $180^\circ$ from the given angle: $210^\circ - 180^\circ = 30^\circ$. The reference angle is the acute angle formed by the terminal side of the angle and the x-axis.

- **Question:** A wheel of radius 0.50 m turns through an angle of 2.4 radians. How far does a point on its rim travel along the arc?

  - **A:** 0.21 m
  - **B:** 2.9 m
  - **C:** 4.8 m
  - **D:** 1.2 m
- **Answer Key:** **D** (1.2 m)

**Explanation:**
The arc length $s$ traveled by a point on the rim of a wheel is given by the formula $s = r\theta$, where $r$ is the radius and $\theta$ is the angle in radians. Substituting the given values, $s = (0.50\ \text{m})(2.4\ \text{rad}) = 1.2\ \text{m}$.

## dost1001-0797

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** Simplify $\frac{1 - \cos^2 x}{\sin x}$, where the expression is defined.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\cos x$
  - **B:** $\sin x$
  - **C:** $\tan x$
  - **D:** $\csc x$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($\sin x$)

**Explanation:**
Using the Pythagorean identity $\sin^2 x + \cos^2 x = 1$, we can rewrite $1 - \cos^2 x$ as $\sin^2 x$. Therefore, the expression becomes $\frac{\sin^2 x}{\sin x}$. Assuming $\sin x \neq 0$, we can simplify this to $\sin x$.

## dost1001-0798

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** Simplify $\tan x \cdot \cot x$, where both functions are defined.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\sin x$
  - **B:** 1
  - **C:** $\cos x$
  - **D:** $\sec x$
- **Answer Key:** **B** (1)

**Explanation:**
The tangent function $\tan x$ and cotangent function $\cot x$ are reciprocals of each other, meaning $\cot x = \frac{1}{\tan x}$. Therefore, their product simplifies to $\tan x \cdot \frac{1}{\tan x} = 1$.

---

## dost1001-0799

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** Which identity is equivalent to $\sec^2 x - \tan^2 x$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\sin^2 x$
  - **B:** $\cos^2 x$
  - **C:** 2
  - **D:** 1
- **Answer Key:** **D** (1)

**Explanation:**
This is one of the fundamental Pythagorean identities in trigonometry. The identity states that $1 + \tan^2 x = \sec^2 x$. Rearranging this identity by subtracting $\tan^2 x$ from both sides gives $\sec^2 x - \tan^2 x = 1$.

---

## dost1001-0800

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** Simplify $\frac{\sec x - \cos x}{\tan x}$, where the expression is defined.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\sin x$
  - **B:** $\cos x$
  - **C:** $\sec x$
  - **D:** $\cot x$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($\sin x$)

**Explanation:**
First, express $\sec x$ and $\tan x$ in terms of $\sin x$ and $\cos x$: $\sec x = \frac{1}{\cos x}$ and $\tan x = \frac{\sin x}{\cos x}$. Substitute these into the expression: $\frac{\frac{1}{\cos x} - \cos x}{\frac{\sin x}{\cos x}} = \frac{\frac{1 - \cos^2 x}{\cos x}}{\frac{\sin x}{\cos x}}$. Simplify the numerator using the identity $1 - \cos^2 x = \sin^2 x$, which yields $\frac{\frac{\sin^2 x}{\cos x}}{\frac{\sin x}{\cos x}}$. Finally, multiply by the reciprocal of the denominator: $\frac{\sin^2 x}{\cos x} \cdot \frac{\cos x}{\sin x} = \sin x$.

---

## dost1001-0801

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** Solve $2\cos x = 1$ for $0 \le x < 2\pi$.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $x = \frac{\pi}{6}$ or $\frac{11\pi}{6}$
  - **B:** $x = \frac{2\pi}{3}$ or $\frac{4\pi}{3}$
  - **C:** $x = \frac{\pi}{2}$ or $\frac{3\pi}{2}$
  - **D:** $x = \frac{\pi}{3}$ or $\frac{5\pi}{3}$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($x = \frac{\pi}{3}$ or $\frac{5\pi}{3}$)

**Explanation:**
First, isolate $\cos x$ by dividing both sides by $2$: $\cos x = \frac{1}{2}$. In the interval $0 \le x < 2\pi$, the cosine function is positive in Quadrants I and IV. The reference angle for which $\cos x = \frac{1}{2}$ is $x = \frac{\pi}{3}$. In Quadrant I, $x = \frac{\pi}{3}$. In Quadrant IV, $x = 2\pi - \frac{\pi}{3} = \frac{6\pi - \pi}{3} = \frac{5\pi}{3}$.

---

## dost1001-0802

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** Solve $\tan\theta = -1$ for $0^\circ \le \theta < 360^\circ$.
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0802.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\theta = 135^\circ$ or $315^\circ$
  - **B:** $\theta = 45^\circ$ or $225^\circ$
  - **C:** $\theta = 135^\circ$ or $225^\circ$
  - **D:** $\theta = 45^\circ$ or $315^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($\theta = 135^\circ$ or $315^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
First, identify the reference angle for which $\tan\theta = 1$, which is $\theta = 45^\circ$. Since $\tan\theta = -1$, the angle $\theta$ must be in Quadrants II or IV, where the tangent function is negative. In Quadrant II, $\theta = 180^\circ - 45^\circ = 135^\circ$. In Quadrant IV, $\theta = 360^\circ - 45^\circ = 315^\circ$.

## dost1001-0803

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** What is the period of $y = \sin(2x)$?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0803.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\pi$
  - **B:** $\frac{\pi}{2}$
  - **C:** $2\pi$
  - **D:** $4\pi$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($\pi$)

**Explanation:**
For a sinusoidal function of the form $y = A\sin(Bx + C) + D$, the period is given by the formula $P = \frac{2\pi}{|B|}$. In the given equation $y = \sin(2x)$, we have $B = 2$. Therefore, the period is $P = \frac{2\pi}{|2|} = \pi$.

---

## dost1001-0804

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** Relative to $y = \cos x$, what horizontal transformation produces $y = \cos\left(x - \frac{\pi}{4}\right)$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** Shift left by $\frac{\pi}{4}$
  - **B:** Shift right by $\frac{\pi}{4}$
  - **C:** Stretch horizontally by factor $\frac{\pi}{4}$
  - **D:** Reflect across the $x$-axis
- **Answer Key:** **B** (Shift right by $\frac{\pi}{4}$)

**Explanation:**
For a function $f(x)$, a transformation of the form $f(x - c)$ represents a horizontal shift to the right by $c$ units, while $f(x + c)$ represents a horizontal shift to the left by $c$ units. In this case, $y = \cos\left(x - \frac{\pi}{4}\right)$ indicates a horizontal shift to the right by $\frac{\pi}{4}$ units compared to $y = \cos x$.

---

## dost1001-0805

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** A sinusoidal graph has maximum 2, minimum $-2$, and one complete cycle from $x = 0$ to $x = 2\pi$. Which equation could represent it if it passes through $(0,0)$ increasing?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0805.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $y = \sin(2x)$
  - **B:** $y = 2\cos x$
  - **C:** $y = 2\sin(2x)$
  - **D:** $y = 2\sin x$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($y = 2\sin x$)

**Explanation:**
The amplitude is half the difference between the maximum and minimum values, so $A = \frac{2 - (-2)}{2} = 2$. One complete cycle from $x=0$ to $x=2\pi$ means the period is $2\pi$, so for $y = A\sin(Bx)$, $B=1$. Since the graph passes through $(0,0)$ and is increasing, it matches the behavior of a sine function, specifically $y = A\sin(Bx)$. Combining these, the equation is $y = 2\sin x$.

---

## dost1001-0806

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** Two sides of a triangle are 5 cm and 7 cm with included angle $60^\circ$. What is the length $c$ of the third side?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0806.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\sqrt{14}\ \text{cm}$
  - **B:** $\sqrt{39}\ \text{cm}$
  - **C:** 6 cm
  - **D:** $\sqrt{74}\ \text{cm}$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($\sqrt{39}\ \text{cm}$)

**Explanation:**
We use the Law of Cosines, $c^2 = a^2 + b^2 - 2ab\cos C$, where $a=5$, $b=7$, and $C=60^\circ$. Substituting these values, $c^2 = 5^2 + 7^2 - 2(5)(7)\cos 60^\circ = 25 + 49 - 70(0.5) = 74 - 35 = 39$. Therefore, $c = \sqrt{39}\ \text{cm}$.

---

## dost1001-0807

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** In triangle $ABC$, $A = 40^\circ$, $a = 8$ cm, and $B = 65^\circ$. What is $b$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\frac{8\sin 65^\circ}{\sin 40^\circ}\ \text{cm}$
  - **B:** $\frac{8\sin 40^\circ}{\sin 65^\circ}\ \text{cm}$
  - **C:** $\frac{8\cos 65^\circ}{\cos 40^\circ}\ \text{cm}$
  - **D:** $\frac{8\tan 65^\circ}{\tan 40^\circ}\ \text{cm}$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($\frac{8\sin 65^\circ}{\sin 40^\circ}\ \text{cm}$)

**Explanation:**
We apply the Law of Sines, which states that $\frac{a}{\sin A} = \frac{b}{\sin B}$. Given $A = 40^\circ$, $a = 8$ cm, and $B = 65^\circ$, we can write $\frac{8}{\sin 40^\circ} = \frac{b}{\sin 65^\circ}$. Solving for $b$, we get $b = \frac{8\sin 65^\circ}{\sin 40^\circ}\ \text{cm}$.

## dost1001-0808

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** A ramp rises 12 m over a horizontal run of 20 m. What is its angle of inclination to the horizontal, to the nearest degree?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0808.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $23^\circ$
  - **B:** $37^\circ$
  - **C:** $59^\circ$
  - **D:** $31^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($31^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
The angle of inclination $\theta$ can be found using the tangent function, as $\tan\theta = \frac{\text{opposite}}{\text{adjacent}} = \frac{\text{rise}}{\text{run}}$. Given a rise of 12 m and a run of 20 m, $\tan\theta = \frac{12}{20} = 0.6$. Therefore, $\theta = \arctan(0.6) \approx 30.96^\circ$, which rounds to $31^\circ$ to the nearest degree.

---

## dost1001-0809

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** A road rises 1 m vertically for every 5 m of horizontal distance. What is its angle of inclination to the horizontal, to the nearest tenth of a degree?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0809.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $11.3^\circ$
  - **B:** $5.7^\circ$
  - **C:** $18.4^\circ$
  - **D:** $78.7^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($11.3^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
The angle of inclination $\theta$ is determined by the tangent function, where $\tan\theta = \frac{\text{vertical rise}}{\text{horizontal distance}}$. Given a 1 m vertical rise for every 5 m horizontal distance, $\tan\theta = \frac{1}{5} = 0.2$. Calculating the inverse tangent, $\theta = \arctan(0.2) \approx 11.3099^\circ$, which rounds to $11.3^\circ$ to the nearest tenth of a degree.

---

## dost1001-0810

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 2
- **Question:** A boat travels 6 km east and then 8 km north. What is the bearing of its resultant displacement, measured east of north?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0810.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** N $53.1^\circ$ E
  - **B:** N $36.9^\circ$ E
  - **C:** S $36.9^\circ$ E
  - **D:** E $36.9^\circ$ N
- **Answer Key:** **B** (N $36.9^\circ$ E)

**Explanation:**
The boat's displacement forms a right triangle with legs 6 km east and 8 km north. The angle $\theta$ measured east of north is given by $\tan\theta = \frac{\text{opposite (east)}}{\text{adjacent (north)}} = \frac{6}{8} = 0.75$. Calculating $\theta = \arctan(0.75) \approx 36.87^\circ$, the bearing is N $36.9^\circ$ E.

## dost1001-0811

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A 10-N force acts at $30^\circ$ above the positive $x$-axis. What are its $x$- and $y$-components?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0811.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $(5\ \text{N},\ 5\sqrt{3}\ \text{N})$
  - **B:** $(10\sqrt{3}\ \text{N},\ 10\ \text{N})$
  - **C:** $(5\sqrt{2}\ \text{N},\ 5\sqrt{2}\ \text{N})$
  - **D:** $(5\sqrt{3}\ \text{N},\ 5\ \text{N})$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($(5\sqrt{3}\ \text{N},\ 5\ \text{N})$)

**Explanation:**
The $x$-component of a force $F$ acting at an angle $\theta$ with the positive $x$-axis is $F_x = F\cos\theta$, and the $y$-component is $F_y = F\sin\theta$. Given $F=10\ \text{N}$ and $\theta=30^\circ$, $F_x = 10\cos 30^\circ = 10(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}) = 5\sqrt{3}\ \text{N}$ and $F_y = 10\sin 30^\circ = 10(\frac{1}{2}) = 5\ \text{N}$.

---

## dost1001-0812

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** An observer's eye is 1.6 m above level ground. The angle of elevation from the observer's eye to the top of a tower is $38^\circ$, and the observer is 25 m from the tower's base. Approximately how tall is the tower?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0812.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 19.5 m
  - **B:** 20.0 m
  - **C:** 21.1 m
  - **D:** 33.6 m
- **Answer Key:** **C** (21.1 m)

**Explanation:**
Let $h$ be the height from the observer's eye level to the top of the tower. Using trigonometry, $\tan 38^\circ = \frac{h}{25\ \text{m}}$, so $h = 25\tan 38^\circ \approx 25(0.7813) \approx 19.53\ \text{m}$. The total height of the tower is the sum of $h$ and the observer's eye height: $19.53\ \text{m} + 1.6\ \text{m} = 21.13\ \text{m}$, which is approximately 21.1 m.

---

## dost1001-0813

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** For an acute angle $\theta$ in a right triangle, $\tan\theta = \frac{5}{12}$. What is $\sec\theta$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\frac{13}{12}$
  - **B:** $\frac{12}{13}$
  - **C:** $\frac{5}{13}$
  - **D:** $\frac{13}{5}$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($\frac{13}{12}$)

**Explanation:**
Given $\tan\theta = \frac{5}{12}$, we can consider a right triangle with an opposite side of 5 and an adjacent side of 12. The hypotenuse $h$ is found using the Pythagorean theorem: $h = \sqrt{5^2 + 12^2} = \sqrt{25 + 144} = \sqrt{169} = 13$. Since $\sec\theta = \frac{\text{hypotenuse}}{\text{adjacent}}$, we have $\sec\theta = \frac{13}{12}$.

---

## dost1001-0814

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** The terminal side of angle $\theta$ passes through the point $(-5, 12)$. What are $\sin\theta$ and $\cos\theta$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\sin\theta = -\frac{5}{13}$, $\cos\theta = \frac{12}{13}$
  - **B:** $\sin\theta = -\frac{12}{13}$, $\cos\theta = \frac{5}{13}$
  - **C:** $\sin\theta = \frac{5}{12}$, $\cos\theta = -\frac{12}{5}$
  - **D:** $\sin\theta = \frac{12}{13}$, $\cos\theta = -\frac{5}{13}$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($\sin\theta = \frac{12}{13}$, $\cos\theta = -\frac{5}{13}$)

**Explanation:**
Given the point $(-5, 12)$, we have $x = -5$ and $y = 12$. The radius $r$ is calculated as $r = \sqrt{x^2 + y^2} = \sqrt{(-5)^2 + (12)^2} = \sqrt{25 + 144} = \sqrt{169} = 13$. Therefore, $\sin\theta = \frac{y}{r} = \frac{12}{13}$ and $\cos\theta = \frac{x}{r} = \frac{-5}{13}$.

---

## dost1001-0815

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A wheel rotates at 120 revolutions per minute. What is its angular speed in radians per second?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0815.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $4\pi\ \text{rad s}^{-1}$
  - **B:** $2\pi\ \text{rad s}^{-1}$
  - **C:** $120\pi\ \text{rad s}^{-1}$
  - **D:** $240\pi\ \text{rad s}^{-1}$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($4\pi\ \text{rad s}^{-1}$)

**Explanation:**
The wheel rotates at $120$ revolutions per minute. To convert this to radians per second, we use the conversion factors $1\ \text{revolution} = 2\pi\ \text{radians}$ and $1\ \text{minute} = 60\ \text{seconds}$. Thus, angular speed $\omega = \frac{120\ \text{revolutions}}{1\ \text{minute}} \times \frac{2\pi\ \text{radians}}{1\ \text{revolution}} \times \frac{1\ \text{minute}}{60\ \text{seconds}} = \frac{120 \times 2\pi}{60}\ \text{rad s}^{-1} = 4\pi\ \text{rad s}^{-1}$.

---

## dost1001-0816

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Simplify $\frac{1 - \cos 2x}{\sin 2x}$, where defined.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\cot x$
  - **B:** $\tan x$
  - **C:** $\sin x$
  - **D:** $2\tan x$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($\tan x$)

**Explanation:**
We use the double angle identities $\cos 2x = 1 - 2\sin^2 x$ and $\sin 2x = 2\sin x \cos x$. Substituting these into the expression gives $\frac{1 - (1 - 2\sin^2 x)}{2\sin x \cos x} = \frac{2\sin^2 x}{2\sin x \cos x}$. Simplifying this expression yields $\frac{\sin x}{\cos x} = \tan x$.

---

## dost1001-0817

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** If $\sin x = \frac{3}{5}$ and $x$ is in Quadrant II, what is $\cos 2x$?
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $-\frac{7}{25}$
  - **B:** $\frac{9}{25}$
  - **C:** $-\frac{16}{25}$
  - **D:** $\frac{7}{25}$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($\frac{7}{25}$)

**Explanation:**
We are given $\sin x = \frac{3}{5}$ and $x$ is in Quadrant II. In Quadrant II, $\cos x$ is negative. Using the identity $\sin^2 x + \cos^2 x = 1$, we find $\cos^2 x = 1 - \left(\frac{3}{5}\right)^2 = 1 - \frac{9}{25} = \frac{16}{25}$, so $\cos x = -\frac{4}{5}$. Now, we use the double angle identity $\cos 2x = \cos^2 x - \sin^2 x = \left(-\frac{4}{5}\right)^2 - \left(\frac{3}{5}\right)^2 = \frac{16}{25} - \frac{9}{25} = \frac{7}{25}$.

---

## dost1001-0818

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Simplify $\frac{\csc x - \sin x}{\cot x}$, where defined.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $\sin x$
  - **B:** $\sec x$
  - **C:** $\cos x$
  - **D:** $\tan x$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($\cos x$)

**Explanation:**
We rewrite the expression in terms of $\sin x$ and $\cos x$: $\frac{\frac{1}{\sin x} - \sin x}{\frac{\cos x}{\sin x}}$. Combining the terms in the numerator gives $\frac{\frac{1 - \sin^2 x}{\sin x}}{\frac{\cos x}{\sin x}}$. Using the identity $1 - \sin^2 x = \cos^2 x$, the expression becomes $\frac{\frac{\cos^2 x}{\sin x}}{\frac{\cos x}{\sin x}}$. Multiplying by the reciprocal of the denominator, we get $\frac{\cos^2 x}{\sin x} \cdot \frac{\sin x}{\cos x} = \cos x$.

---

## dost1001-0819

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Simplify $\frac{1}{1 - \sin x} + \frac{1}{1 + \sin x}$, where defined.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $2\sec^2 x$
  - **B:** $2\csc^2 x$
  - **C:** $\sec^2 x$
  - **D:** $2\tan^2 x$
- **Answer Key:** **A** ($2\sec^2 x$)

**Explanation:**
To simplify the expression, we find a common denominator: $\frac{1}{1 - \sin x} + \frac{1}{1 + \sin x} = \frac{(1 + \sin x) + (1 - \sin x)}{(1 - \sin x)(1 + \sin x)}$. The numerator simplifies to $1 + \sin x + 1 - \sin x = 2$. The denominator is a difference of squares: $(1 - \sin x)(1 + \sin x) = 1 - \sin^2 x$. Using the identity $1 - \sin^2 x = \cos^2 x$, the expression becomes $\frac{2}{\cos^2 x}$. Since $\frac{1}{\cos x} = \sec x$, the simplified expression is $2\sec^2 x$.

---

## dost1001-0820

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Solve $2\sin^2 x - 1 = 0$ for $0 \le x < 2\pi$.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $x = \frac{\pi}{4}, \frac{5\pi}{4}$ only
  - **B:** $x = \frac{\pi}{2}, \frac{3\pi}{2}$
  - **C:** $x = \frac{\pi}{4}, \frac{3\pi}{4}, \frac{5\pi}{4}, \frac{7\pi}{4}$
  - **D:** $x = 0, \pi$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($x = \frac{\pi}{4}, \frac{3\pi}{4}, \frac{5\pi}{4}, \frac{7\pi}{4}$)

**Explanation:**
We solve the equation $2\sin^2 x - 1 = 0$ for $\sin x$. This gives $2\sin^2 x = 1$, so $\sin^2 x = \frac{1}{2}$. Taking the square root of both sides, $\sin x = \pm\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} = \pm\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}$. For $\sin x = \frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}$, the solutions in the interval $0 \le x < 2\pi$ are $x = \frac{\pi}{4}$ (Quadrant I) and $x = \frac{3\pi}{4}$ (Quadrant II). For $\sin x = -\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}$, the solutions are $x = \frac{5\pi}{4}$ (Quadrant III) and $x = \frac{7\pi}{4}$ (Quadrant IV).

---

## dost1001-0821

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Solve $\sin(2x) = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$ for $0 \le x < 2\pi$.
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $x = \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{4\pi}{3}$ only
  - **B:** $x = \frac{\pi}{6}, \frac{5\pi}{6}$
  - **C:** $x = \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{2\pi}{3}, \frac{4\pi}{3}, \frac{5\pi}{3}$
  - **D:** $x = \frac{\pi}{6}, \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{7\pi}{6}, \frac{4\pi}{3}$
- **Answer Key:** **D** ($x = \frac{\pi}{6}, \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{7\pi}{6}, \frac{4\pi}{3}$)

**Explanation:**
We need to solve $\sin(2x) = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$. The general solutions for $\sin \theta = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$ are $\theta = \frac{\pi}{3} + 2k\pi$ and $\theta = \frac{2\pi}{3} + 2k\pi$, where $k$ is an integer. Substituting $2x$ for $\theta$, we have $2x = \frac{\pi}{3} + 2k\pi$ and $2x = \frac{2\pi}{3} + 2k\pi$. Dividing by $2$, we get $x = \frac{\pi}{6} + k\pi$ and $x = \frac{\pi}{3} + k\pi$. For $k=0$, $x = \frac{\pi}{6}$ and $x = \frac{\pi}{3}$. For $k=1$, $x = \frac{\pi}{6} + \pi = \frac{7\pi}{6}$ and $x = \frac{\pi}{3} + \pi = \frac{4\pi}{3}$. These are all solutions within $0 \le x < 2\pi$.

---

## dost1001-0822

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Solve $2\cos^2 x - 3\cos x + 1 = 0$ for $0 \le x < 2\pi$.
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0822.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $x = \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{5\pi}{3}$ only
  - **B:** $x = 0, \frac{2\pi}{3}, \frac{4\pi}{3}$
  - **C:** $x = 0, \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{5\pi}{3}$
  - **D:** $x = \pi, \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{5\pi}{3}$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($x = 0, \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{5\pi}{3}$)

**Explanation:**
Let $u = \cos x$. The equation becomes $2u^2 - 3u + 1 = 0$, which factors as $(2u - 1)(u - 1) = 0$. This yields $u = \frac{1}{2}$ or $u = 1$. Substituting back, $\cos x = \frac{1}{2}$ gives $x = \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{5\pi}{3}$ in the interval $[0, 2\pi)$, and $\cos x = 1$ gives $x = 0$. Thus, the solutions are $x = 0, \frac{\pi}{3}, \frac{5\pi}{3}$.

---

## dost1001-0823

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A sinusoidal graph has midline $y = 1$, maximum 3, minimum $-1$, and period $\pi$. It passes through $(0,1)$ increasing. Which equation fits the graph?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0823.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $y = 1 + 2\sin x$
  - **B:** $y = 1 + 2\sin(2x)$
  - **C:** $y = 2 + \sin(2x)$
  - **D:** $y = 1 + 2\cos(2x)$
- **Answer Key:** **B** ($y = 1 + 2\sin(2x)$)

**Explanation:**
The midline is $y=1$, so the vertical shift is $D=1$. The amplitude is half the difference between maximum and minimum values, $A = \frac{3 - (-1)}{2} = \frac{4}{2} = 2$. The period is $\pi$, so $B = \frac{2\pi}{\text{period}} = \frac{2\pi}{\pi} = 2$. Since the graph passes through $(0,1)$ increasing, it behaves like a sine function with no horizontal shift, so the equation is $y = D + A\sin(Bx) = 1 + 2\sin(2x)$.

---

## dost1001-0824

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** Which description matches $y = -2\sin\left(x + \frac{\pi}{2}\right)$?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0824.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** Amplitude 2, shifted right $\frac{\pi}{2}$ with no reflection
  - **B:** Amplitude 2, reflected across the $x$-axis, shifted left $\frac{\pi}{2}$
  - **C:** Amplitude $\frac{1}{2}$, shifted left $\frac{\pi}{2}$
  - **D:** Amplitude 2, reflected across the $y$-axis only
- **Answer Key:** **B** (Amplitude 2, reflected across the $x$-axis, shifted left $\frac{\pi}{2}$)

**Explanation:**
For $y = -2\sin\left(x + \frac{\pi}{2}\right)$, the amplitude is $|-2| = 2$. The negative sign indicates a reflection across the $x$-axis. The term $\left(x + \frac{\pi}{2}\right)$ indicates a horizontal shift of $\frac{\pi}{2}$ units to the left.

---

## dost1001-0825

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** A triangle has side lengths 7 cm, 8 cm, and 9 cm. What is the angle opposite the 9-cm side, to the nearest tenth of a degree?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0825.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** $60.0^\circ$
  - **B:** $65.4^\circ$
  - **C:** $73.4^\circ$
  - **D:** $106.6^\circ$
- **Answer Key:** **C** ($73.4^\circ$)

**Explanation:**
Let the sides be $a=7$, $b=8$, and $c=9$. We need to find the angle $C$ opposite side $c$. Using the Law of Cosines, $c^2 = a^2 + b^2 - 2ab\cos C$. Substituting the values, $9^2 = 7^2 + 8^2 - 2(7)(8)\cos C$, which simplifies to $81 = 49 + 64 - 112\cos C$. This gives $81 = 113 - 112\cos C$, so $112\cos C = 113 - 81 = 32$. Therefore, $\cos C = \frac{32}{112} = \frac{2}{7}$. Calculating $C = \arccos\left(\frac{2}{7}\right) \approx 73.398^\circ$, which is $73.4^\circ$ to the nearest tenth of a degree.

---

## dost1001-0826

- **Topic:** trigonometry | **Difficulty Level:** 3
- **Question:** In triangle $ABC$, $A = 30^\circ$, $a = 10$ cm, and $b = 12$ cm. How many distinct triangles satisfy these data?
- **Image:** `dost1001-q0826.png`
- **Choices:**
  - **A:** 0
  - **B:** 1
  - **C:** 2
  - **D:** 3
- **Answer Key:** **C** (2)

**Explanation:**
This is an ambiguous case of the Law of Sines. We are given angle $A=30^\circ$, side $a=10$, and side $b=12$. First, calculate the height $h = b\sin A = 12\sin 30^\circ = 12(0.5) = 6$. Since $h < a < b$ (i.e., $6 < 10 < 12$), there are two distinct triangles that satisfy the given conditions.
